EXPECTED : DERMAL : Intradermal Injection and Occlusive Patch

           days/week for up to 104 weeks [9].

           Regarding effects that changed the NOAEL or classification hazard,
           the provided text does not mention a NOAEL (No Observed Adverse

           Information and Research Center (SIRC) has not made a determination

           substance presents a substantial risk of injury to human health or
           the environment [2].

           Regarding specific health effects observed in the animals:
           * **Degeneration:** Slight degeneration of epithelial cells lining
           the terminal bronchioles was observed in CD-1 mice and WT (C57BL/6)
           mice [5].
           * **Hyperplasia:** Hyperplasia was observed in the terminal
           bronchioles of CD-1 mice and WT mice [4].
           * **Tumorigenicity:** Styrene increased the incidence of
           bronchioloalveolar adenomas and carcinomas in CD-1 mice [5].
           * **Cell Proliferation:** Increased cell proliferation occurred in
           CD-1 and WT mice exposed to 120 ppm styrene for 1 week [8].

           The document argues that these effects are due to mouse-specific
           CYP2F2 metabolism, which is a rodent mode of action that the
           authors claim is not relevant to humans [2].

[Q 11/11]  chat 275  1.6s
PROMPT   : regarding 1.3_Oral_repd, answer the following: Answer this question
           only if the study is a repeated dose study. Otherwise, answer 'not
           applicable'. What effects on the test animals changed the NOAEL or
           the classification hazard? This could be changes in food
           consumption, organ weight change, weight change, or any other
           health problem observed in the animal due to the substance. If
           there were none, answer "null".
EXPECTED : APPROPRIATE FOOD CONSUMPTION : Significant reduction in appetite
           <br>BODY WEIGHT CHANGE : Decrease in body weight gain, with
           affected rats reaching approximately half the body weight of
           control animals by study's end <br>ORGAN WEIGHT CHANGES: Changes in
           liver and kidney weights compared to controls <br>HEALTH PROBLEMS
           OBSERVED: Histopathological changes observed specifically in testis
ANSWER   : The study is a repeated dose study, as it involved feeding rats
           butyl CELLOSOLVE in their diets for three months [1].

           The following effects were observed at the 1.25% dosage level,
           which influenced the study's findings:
           * **Appetite/Diet Consumption:** Diet consumption was significantly
           depressed in both male and female rats [2].
           * **Body Weight:** Body weight gain was depressed to about one-half
           that of the control group [2].
           * **Organ Weights:** Mean liver and kidney weights were increased
           [2].
           * **Histopathology:** Testicular atrophy was observed and verified
           microscopically [8].

           At the 0.25% dosage level, the following effects were observed:
           * **Appetite/Diet Consumption:** Appetite was depressed in male
           rats [2].
           * **Body Weight:** Weight gain was depressed in male rats [2].
           * **Histopathology:** Testicular atrophy was observed and verified
           microscopically [8].

           The study concluded that the 0.05% dosage level was without
           deleterious effect on the criteria measured [9].

[Q 1/11]  chat 276  1.4s
PROMPT   : regarding 1.4_Vitro_Geno, answer the following: Determine the
           exposure type (ORAL, DERMAL, or INHALATION) for the toxicology
           study, then classify the exposure method.
EXPECTED : Not applicable
ANSWER   : The provided documents do not state whether the exposure type for
           the toxicology study was oral, dermal, or inhalation. The study is
           an *in vitro* "Reverse Mutation Assay" (Ames Test) using bacterial
           strains, which involves exposing bacteria to the test item in a
           laboratory setting rather than administering it to a living
           organism via oral, dermal, or inhalation routes [1, 3, 9!].

           Regarding the exposure method, the test item was applied using the
           "Ames plate incorporation and pre-incubation methods" [9!]. This
           involved mixing the test item formulation with bacterial culture,
           molten top agar, and either S9-mix or phosphate buffer, then
           overlaying the mixture onto sterile plates of Vogel-Bonner Minimal
           agar [2!].

           in absolute weights of the liver, kidneys, spleen at higher doses
           (LOAEL);<br>- Relative splenic-to-body weight ratios were notably
           lower at 3000 ppm.
ANSWER   : The study is a repeated dose study, as it involved a 13-week
           subchronic inhalation toxicity study with repeated exposure [1].

           The no-observed-effect level (NOEL) for this study is considered to
           be 500 ppm [4]. The effects observed at higher concentrations (1500
           and 3000 ppm) that informed this determination include:

           * **Respiratory tract irritation:** Signs of upper respiratory
           tract irritation were observed in the nasal passages of animals
           exposed to 1500 and 3000 ppm, including necrosis of the olfactory
           epithelium [7].
           * **Reduced activity:** Animals exposed to 1500 and 3000 ppm
           displayed acute, transient signs of reduced activity levels during
           exposure [7].
           * **Body weight and feed consumption:** The 1500 and 3000 ppm
           treatments produced decreased body weight and feed consumption
           throughout much of the exposure period [3]. While the 500 ppm
           treatment also resulted in reductions in feed consumption at a few
           time periods, this was not accompanied by statistically significant
           reductions in body weight [3].
           * **Stomach irritation:** Gross necropsy and microscopic
           observations showed signs of irritation to the glandular stomach
           and necrosis in the non-glandular stomach in some 3000 ppm female
           rats [3].

           Despite these findings, the study concluded there was no systemic
           or organ-specific toxicity [4].

           results)
ANSWER   : The maximum dose level of Nigrosine Base EX tested was 2500 µg/ml
           [2]. However, due to the formation of precipitates and large clumps
           of test material, the maximum *practical* dose level used in the 6
           (18) and 24-hour treatment groups was 625 µg/ml [2]. In the 48-hour
           treatment group, the maximum dose level was limited to 78.1 µg/ml
           due to toxicity [2].

PROMPT   : regarding 1.9_Inhalation_Repd, answer the following: Answer this
           question only if the study is a repeated dose study. Otherwise,
           answer 'not applicable'. What effects on the test animals changed
           the NOAEL or the classification hazard? This could be changes in
           food consumption, organ weight change, weight change, or any other
           health problem observed in the animal due to the substance. If
           there were none, answer "null".
EXPECTED : - Changes in food consumption : Both mid- and high-exposure male
           groups showed reduced food consumption throughout the study
           compared to controls.<br><br>- Weight change (body weight
           reduction) : Mean body weights of mid- and high-exposure males were
           significantly lower than those of control group males by 9.2% and
           13.3%, respectively, at terminal necropsy (study week 13).<br><br>-
           Organ weight change (epididymides atrophy): Mild multifocal atrophy
           in the epididymides observed particularly in males from both
           exposure periods, with more pronounced effects noted by week 13 for
           higher exposures.
ANSWER   : The study described in document 1.9 is a repeated dose study, as it
           involved 13 weeks of exposure (at least 65 exposures) [1].

           The following effects were observed in the test animals, which
           contributed to the finding that a No Observable Effect Level (NOEL)
           could not be established for tetrahydrofurfuryl alcohol (THFA) [1]:

           * **Clinical Signs:** Intermittent whole-body spasms were observed
           frequently in a dose-related manner in all exposed groups [2].
           Hyperactivity was noted one-hour post-exposure in all test groups
           [2]. Occasional hypoactivity and excessive grooming occurred in the
           high exposure group [2]. Wet yellow urogenital matting and a low
           incidence of salivation were noted in the high exposure group [2].
           * **Body Weight and Food Consumption:** Mean body weights were
           decreased in mid- and high-exposure group males, with terminal
           necropsy values 9.2% and 13.3% lower than controls, respectively
           [5]. Mean food consumption was lower in mid- and high-exposure
           group males throughout the study [5].
           * **Hematology:** Decreased platelet and hemoglobin means were
           observed in high exposure group males and females at study weeks
           three and 13 [5]. Decreased Mean Corpuscular Hemoglobin (MCH)
           values were noted in high exposure group males at week three, and
           in both sexes of the high exposure group at week 13 [5].
           * **Organ Weights and Pathology:**
           * **Testes/Reproductive:** Mean absolute seminal vesicle weight,
           and absolute and relative epididymides weights were decreased in
           high exposure group males [4]. Mean absolute and relative prostate
           weights were decreased in both mid- and high-exposure groups [5].
           Microscopic examination revealed mild multifocal atrophy of the
           testes in one high exposure group male [5]. Decreased epididymal
           sperm numbers and sperm motility, as well as an increased incidence
           of morphologically abnormal sperm, were observed in high exposure
           group males at weeks six and 13 [5].

[Q 11/11]  chat 374  1.7s
PROMPT   : regarding 2.2_Dermal_Repd, answer the following: Answer this
           question only if the study is a repeated dose study. Otherwise,
           answer 'not applicable'. What effects on the test animals changed
           the NOAEL or the classification hazard? This could be changes in
           food consumption, organ weight change, weight change, or any other
           health problem observed in the animal due to the substance. If
           there were none, answer "null".
EXPECTED : CRITICAL EFFECTS : Lower mean body weights and weight gains;
           Decreased spermatogenesis (sperm count, production rate, motility).
           <br>NOAEL DETERMINATION: 100 mg/kg/day for male rats and 300
           mg/kg/day for female rats. <br><br>Critical Effects Influencing
           NOAEL or Classification Hazard:<br><br>- \*\*Lower Mean Body
           Weights and Weight Gains\*\*: Observed at a dose of 1000 mg/kg/day
           in both males and females over the course of 13 weeks, indicating
           systemic toxicity.<br> <br>- \*\*Decreased Spermatogenesis (Sperm
           Count, Production Rate, Motility)\*\*: Noted particularly in male
           rats treated with THFA dermal application. Decreases were observed
           at doses of 300 and 1000 mg/kg/day for sperm count and production
           rate; motility decrease specifically noted at the higher
           dose.<br><br>These effects are pivotal as they directly influenced
           NOAEL determination, highlighting systemic toxicity
           thresholds:<br><br>- \*\*NOAEL Determination\*\*: Identified to be
           100 mg/kg/day for male rats based on decreased spermatogenesis and
           lower body weight metrics. For female rats, it was determined to be
           300 mg/kg/day after considering the adverse outcomes of reduced
           mean body weights.<br>
ANSWER   : The study described in `2.2_Dermal_repd.pdf` is a 13-week
           subchronic dermal toxicity study involving 65 applications, which
           qualifies as a repeated dose study [4].

           The following effects were observed in the test animals that
           influenced the determination of the no observable effect level
           (NOEL) for systemic toxicity:

           * **Body Weight:** Following 13 consecutive weeks of treatment,
           lower mean body weights and body weight gains were observed in both
           males and females at the 1000 mg/kg/day dose level [1].
           * **Spermatogenesis:** An adverse effect on spermatogenesis was
           noted after 13 weeks of administration. Specifically, the mean
           number of sperm in the testis and the mean sperm production rate
           decreased in males in the 300 and 1000 mg/kg/day groups, and a
           decrease in the mean percentage of motile sperm was noted in the
           1000 mg/kg/day group males [1].

           Based on these findings, the NOEL for systemic toxicity was
           determined to be 100 mg/kg/day for males and 300 mg/kg/day for
           females [3].

ANSWER   : The studies described in `2.4_Oral_Repd.pdf` are repeated dose
           studies, as they involve daily oral gavage administration of 6PPD
           to rats over a period of weeks (e.g., postnatal day 22 to 42/43 for
           females and postnatal day 23 to 53/54 for males) [3, 8!].

           Regarding the effects observed in the female rat study, the
           following changes were noted:

           * **Body weight:** Initial reduced body weight gain (first two days
           of dosing) and mean body weight on PND 24 and 25, and reduced body
           weight at the time of vaginal opening [7].
           * **Organ weights:** Increased absolute and relative liver weight,
           increased relative kidney weight, decreased absolute and relative
           pituitary weight, and decreased absolute ovarian and uterine
           weights [7].
           * **Hormonal and clinical chemistry:** Increased serum TSH,
           non-statistically significant decrease in serum T4, increased serum
           cholesterol, total protein, and globulin, and decreased serum
           triglycerides [7].
           * **Other health/developmental parameters:** Increased thyroid
           follicular cell height and decreased colloid area, and a decrease
           in the percentage of regularly cycling animals [7].

           The provided text does not explicitly state which of these specific
           effects resulted in a change to a NOAEL (No Observed Adverse Effect
           Level) or a change in classification hazard. Instead, the document
           notes that the WBCSD/TIP has not made a determination that these
           results constitute reliable evidence of a significant risk of
           injury to human health or the environment, and therefore, it was
           not submitted as a required report under TSCA Section 8(e) [1]. It
           further states that some of these effects (liver and kidney
           weights, serum thyroid hormone, testosterone in males, and clinical

PROMPT   : regarding 2.7_Vitro_Geno, answer the following: Determine the
           exposure type (ORAL, DERMAL, or INHALATION) for the toxicology
           study, then classify the exposure method.
EXPECTED : In vitro / Not applicable
ANSWER   : The provided document corpus does not state whether the exposure
           type was oral, dermal, or inhalation. The study is an "IN VITRO
           MAMMALIAN CHROMOSOME ABERRATION TEST" [3], which involves exposing
           CHO (Chinese Hamster Ovary) cells to a test article in a laboratory
           setting rather than administering it to a living organism via oral,
           dermal, or inhalation routes [3].

           Regarding the exposure method, the cells were treated by refeeding
           flasks with complete medium (for non-activated exposure) or an S9
           reaction mixture (for S9-activated exposure), to which a dosing
           solution of the test article or solvent was added [10]. The
           treatment occurred at 37± 1°C in a humidified atmosphere of 5± 1%
           CO2 in air [10].

[Q 1/11]  chat 430  1.1s
PROMPT   : regarding 2.8_Dermal_Sens, answer the following: Determine the
           exposure type (ORAL, DERMAL, or INHALATION) for the toxicology
           study, then classify the exposure method.
EXPECTED : Dermal: Topical Application
ANSWER   : The toxicology study for Dihexyl ether is a dermal exposure study
           [5!]. The study assesses the skin sensitization potential of the
           substance following its "dermal application to mice" [5!].

           Regarding the exposure method, the study utilizes the Local Lymph
           Node Assay (LLNA) [5!]. This method involves measuring the
           proliferation of lymphocytes isolated from the auricular lymph
           nodes (the lymph nodes draining the site of exposure, which is the
           dorsal aspect of the ears) in mice [9!].
